國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國財政壓力仍高，房市降溫與通膨回升增添經濟不確定性 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1,668 億美元，年減 51.6%，主因收入增
加、支出下降，但部分支出提前至7 月支付，影響單月表現。累計前11
個月赤字仍近 2 兆美元，關稅收入轉正，惟利息支出持續攀升，財政壓
力仍高。 
⚫ 8 月美國成屋銷售年率降至 398 萬戶，月減 2.0%、年減 1.2%，為 2025
年 6 月以來首度跌破 400 萬戶，顯示高房貸利率持續抑制房市需求。房
價中位數為 42.91 萬美元，年增 1.6%，連續 38 個月上漲；待售成屋庫
存增至 162 萬戶，相當於 4.9 個月供給量，創逾 10 年高點。整體而言，
成屋市場呈現銷售放緩、房價續升與庫存增加態勢，供給改善有助市場
逐步趨於平衡。 
⚫ 8 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)月增 0.4%，高於上月 0.1%，年增率維持
3.4%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，年增率則降至 2.4%。能源價格由跌轉升，
其中汽油上漲 3.9%， 住房月增0.3%， 為推升通膨主因 ； 食品月增0.1%。
整體而言，短期物價壓力升溫，但核心通膨仍持續降溫。 
⚫ 8 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)月增 0.4%、年增 5.4%，高於上月的 0.1%
及 4.8%，主要反映能源價格回升影響，其中柴油價格大漲24.1%；服務
價格則受運輸及倉儲成本增加推升而續呈上揚。核心 PPI 月增 0.3%，
低於前月的 0.4%，顯示生產端通膨壓力仍具韌性，惟基礎通膨動能尚
未明顯升溫。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期美加貿易衝突持續升溫 ， 加拿大自9 月 8 日起對美國商品加徵 15%
至 50%報復性關稅 ；美國則擴大限制部分加拿大乳製品 、酒類及機車產
品輸美，並限制加拿大企業參與部分政府採購，雙方爭端已由關稅戰延
伸至市場准入領域，增添北美供應鏈及貿易前景不確定性。 
⚫ 美國國會推進對俄羅斯新制裁法案 ， 擬授權總統對俄羅斯能源主要購買
國商品課徵最高 100%關稅，以壓縮俄方能源收入。法案雖獲參議院高
票支持，但眾議院前景仍不明，且可能波及中國、印度及部分盟友，引
發貿易與外交疑慮。 
短評 ⚫ 美國財政赤字雖因付款時點移轉而在 8 月明顯縮減 ， 但累計赤字仍接近

品依賴國際供應鏈支持。

115.9.14 
一、 美國—財政壓力仍高，房市降溫與通膨回升增添經濟不確定性 
2026 年 8 月(2026 財年第 11 個月)美國聯邦政府財政赤字約 1,668.0 億美元，
較去年同期 3,447.9 億美元縮減 51.6%；當月政府收入約 3,600.3 億美元，年增
4.6%，政府支出約 5,268.3 億美元，年減23.5%，惟部分原定 8 月初支付的軍人薪
資與退休金、退伍軍人福利、補充保障收入及部分醫療保險給付款項因 8 月 1 日
適逢非工作日而提前至 7 月支付，使單月支出受到付款時點影響。 收入方面，8
月關稅淨收入約 128.4 億美元，扭轉 7 月關稅淨收入為負 85.5 億美元的情況；累
計前 11 個月關稅淨收入約 1,673.1 億美元，較去年同期增加 1.3%。 支出方面，
8 月淨利息支出約 856.1 億美元，累計前 11 個月達約 1 兆 169.7 億美元，年增
8.9%，顯示利息負擔持續增加。 累計 2026 財年前 11 個月，美國聯邦政府財政
赤字約 1 兆 9,655.9 億美元，較去年同期小幅縮減 0.4%，其中收入年增 3.3%至約
4 兆 8,454.5 億美元，支出年增 2.2%至約 6 兆 8,110.4 億美元。整體而言，8 月單
月赤字雖明顯縮減 ， 但部分受到付款時點移轉影響 ， 且累計赤字仍接近2 兆美元 ，
加上利息支出持續攀升，美國聯邦財政壓力仍維持高檔。 
2026 年 8 月美國成屋銷售經季節調整後年率降至 398 萬戶 ， 較上月減少2.0%，
較去年同期下降 1.2%，並為 2025 年 6 月以來首次跌破 400 萬戶，顯示高房貸利
率持續抑制房市交易動能。區域表現方面，西部銷售持平，東北部、中西部及南
部分別月減 4.0%、3.1%及 1.6%；房價方面，成屋售價中位數為42.91 萬美元，年
增 1.6%，連續 38 個月上漲。供給端則持續改善，待售成屋庫存增至 162 萬戶，
月增 3.2%、年增 5.9%，相當於 4.9 個月供給量，為近 10 多年來最高水準，住房
可負擔指數亦由去年同期的 101.2 升至 104.7。整體而言，8 月美國成屋市場呈現
銷售放緩、房價續漲及庫存增加的格局，高利率仍抑制成交動能，惟供給增加及
可負擔性改善，有助提升購屋者的選擇與議價空間，並促進市場逐步趨於平衡。 
2026 年 8 月美國消費者物價指數(CPI)經季節調整後月增 0.4%，高於上月的
0.1%，年增率維持 3.4%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%，高於前月的 0.2%，但年增率由
2.5%降至 2.4%。在細項方面，能源價格由跌轉升，能源指數月增 2.1%，其中汽
油價格上漲 3.9%，成為推升整體物價的主要因素；住房指數月增 0.3%，食品指

數月增 0.1%。此外，通訊、外宿住宿、機票、教育及車輛價格均呈上漲，醫療照
護及汽車保險價格則下跌。整體而言，8 月美國通膨回升主要受汽油與住房價格
上漲帶動，短期物價壓力有所增強；惟核心 CPI 年增率續降，顯示基礎通膨仍維
持降溫趨勢。 
2026 年 8 月美國生產者物價指數(PPI)經季節調整後月增 0.4%，高於上月之
0.1%，年增率則由上月的 4.8%升至 5.4%。其中最終需求商品價格月增 1.1%，結
束前兩個月下跌走勢 ， 超過四分之三的漲幅係由能源價格月增4.2%所帶動 ； 細項
中，柴油價格大漲 24.1%，汽油、航空燃油及燃料油價格亦同步上升，食品價格
則微增 0.1%。另一方面，最終需求服務價格月增 0.1%，已連續第三個月上升，
主要受運輸及倉儲服務價格上漲 2.3%支撐 ， 其中貨車貨運服務及航空客運服務價
格分別上漲 2.0%及 4.2%，惟批發及零售業利潤率下滑，使部分服務價格漲幅受
到抵銷。此外，剔除食品、能源及貿易服務後之核心最終需求PPI 月增 0.3%，低
於 7 月之 0.4%，年增率維持 4.7%。整體而言，8 月 PPI 回升主要反映能源價格反
彈及運輸成本上升影響，顯示美國生產端通膨壓力仍具韌性；惟核心 PPI 增幅較
前月略為放緩，基礎通膨動能尚未進一步升溫，後續仍須持續關注能源價格波動
及服務成本變化對整體物價走勢之影響。 
近期美加貿易衝突持續升溫，雙方關稅報復行動已進一步擴大至非關稅措施
領域 。 加拿大自9 月 8 日起正式對約 200 億美元以上美國商品課徵 15%至 50%不
等的報復性關稅，涵蓋鋼鐵、鋁製品、家電、服飾及農業設備等產品，以回應美
國先前對加拿大商品加徵關稅措施 。 面對加拿大反制 ， 美國政府隨即宣布擴大限
制措施，除關稅措施外，將自 9 月 29 日起限制部分加拿大乳製品、酒類及機車
產品輸美，並限制加拿大企業參與部分美國政府採購標案，使雙方爭端由關稅戰
延伸至市場准入及政府採購領域。 加拿大總理 Mark Carney 表示，美國措施可
能衝擊加拿大關鍵產業發展，加方將持續採取對等回應措施。市場亦關注美方後
續可能進一步提高加拿大汽車、鋼鐵及相關零組件關稅，增添北美供應鏈及企業
投資規劃不確定性。整體而言，美加經貿關係短期內仍難見明顯緩和，後續發展
恐進一步增添北美及全球貿易前景不確定性。 
美國國會近期推進針對俄羅斯的新制裁法案，內容包括授權總統對俄羅斯原
油或天然氣主要購買國的進口商品課徵最高 100%關稅，以進一步限制俄羅斯能
源收入並加大對俄施壓 。 該法案已獲參議院以高票通過 ， 眾議院則預計展開審議 ，

但通過前景仍具不確定性。部分民主黨議員認為，相關條款可能過度擴張總統關
稅裁量權，且中國、印度及部分美國盟友均可能受到影響，引發對貿易關係、消
費成本及外交後果的疑慮，因此後續立法進展及實際執行範圍仍有待觀察。 
二、歐元區—歐洲央行如期升息一碼，且仍有繼續升息的空間 
 歐元區第二季經濟成長季率終值由初值上修 0.2 個百分點至 0.6%，但上修主
因來自愛爾蘭經濟大幅成長 10.2%；若排除愛爾蘭，則歐元區第二季經濟成長季
率僅約 0.3%。從需求面觀察，第二季消費貢獻 0.2 個百分點，雖然通膨上升侵蝕
購買力，但薪資漲幅仍優於預期，加上高儲蓄率支持，使消費動能得以延續。其
中 ， 服務消費維持穩健成長 ， 耐久財支出亦明顯增加 。 另方面 ， 淨出口貢獻0.9 個
百分點，但設備投資下滑使整體固定投資表現仍顯疲弱。 
 歐洲央行 9 月 10 日如期宣布升息一碼，存款機制利率調升為 2.5%，但仍維
持「依經濟數據逐次決策」的立場。值得注意的是，本次歐洲央行分別上修2026
年、2027 年經濟成長率 0.1、0.2 個百分點至 0.9%、1.4%，以及 2028 年核心通膨
上修 0.1 個百分點至 2.3%， 反映歐洲央行認為經濟尚具韌性 ， 即便通膨壓力增溫 ，
仍能減輕其負面影響，使得政策利率繼續上調的空間，故一般預期歐洲央行將會
在 10 月政策會議採取觀望，並在12 月宣布升息一碼，2027 年第一季升息機率亦
有所上升，歐洲央行貨幣政策短期內仍將維持偏鷹立場。 
三、日本—內閣府上修第二季經濟成長，薪資動能續強 
2026 年第二季經濟成長率由 1.1%上修至 1.4%，主要受國內需求及庫存
貢獻改善帶動，其中以資本支出修正為國內需求上修的主因。展望下半年，
在政府財政刺激措施、企業持續擴大設備投資及薪資成長帶動消費支出的支
撐下，日本經濟可望維持溫和成長態勢，惟外部需求復甦力道、能源價格變
動及地緣政治風險仍為影響經濟表現的不確定因素。 
7 月薪資年增 4.7%，優於市場預期，其中夏季獎金年增 6.3%為主要推升
因素；經常性薪資及兼職時薪分別年增 4.1%及 5.7%，增速均較前期擴大 ， 顯
示薪資成長動能持續增強。此外，實質薪資年增3.4%，反映勞工購買力進一
步改善。在企業獲利表現穩健支撐下，儘管仍面臨成本上升及外部環境不確
定性 ， 企業仍維持獎金發放與加薪趨勢 ， 有助支撐民間消費韌性及經濟表現 。

### Page 8

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

7

四、中國—進出口同步強勁成長，貿易順差增幅則相對有限 
8 月出口年增率較 7 月回升 1.1 個百分點至 25%，出口總值為 4,014 億美元；
累計前八個月出口年增率為 19.3%，累積出口總值為 29,238 億美元。前八個月
IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器等)累積出口額分別成長 103.9%、49.4%；船
舶累積出口成長 35.2%；汽車累積出口成長 53.2%。另方面，8 月進口年增率較 7
月回升 0.7 個百分點至 28.2%， 進口總值為2,824 億美元 ； 累計前八個月進口值成
長 28.2%至 21,183 億美元，其中 IC、自動數據處理設備(包含伺服器等)進口分別
成長 61.7%、105.1%，呼應中國對高階 IC 的依賴，以及部分伺服器關鍵組件有賴
於進口滿足所需。進出口相抵後，前八個月貿易順差達 8,055 億美元，僅較去年
同期增加 2.6%， 增幅明顯低於出口 ， 顯示出口擴張同時伴隨關鍵原物料進口大幅
增加，稀釋出口成長的加值效果，亦反映部分高附加價值產品依賴國際供應鏈支
持。 
8 月生產者通膨(出廠價格)由 7 月的 3.5%回升至 3.8%，主因原油價格自 7 月
起走升，其影響逐漸反映於上游生產成本。當月石油和天然氣開採業出廠價格年
增率反彈 7.3 個百分點至 10.5%，並帶動燃料加工業通膨上揚，抵銷近期鋼鐵價
格下滑，導致相關礦產開採及冶煉加工產品價格較去年同期下滑的影響。此外，
對於原油價格波動較為敏感的化學工業、化纖產業通膨沒有明顯變動，部分可能
與先前油價上漲期間累積較高庫存有關，惟在近期油價再度走升下，不排除相關
成本壓力於未來數月逐步反映。另因金價盤整，有色金屬礦採選業出廠價格年增
率亦持續放慢。 
8 月消費者通膨(CPI)回升 0.3 個百分點為 0.8%，但仍然是近七個月的次低水
準，核心通膨(不含食品及能源)回升 0.1 個百分點至 1 %。當月受國際原油價格上
漲影響，燃油通膨反彈 7.5 個百分點至 8.3%；手機零組件上漲，通信工具通膨上
揚 2.3 個百分點至 10.6%；夏季旅遊需求支持旅遊服務通膨增加 0.1 個百分點至
2.7%；其他商品及服務價格則大致平穩。整體而言，8 月能源價格有所回升，但
在企業庫存水位仍偏高情況下，抑制成本的傳導速度，除燃油價格外，尚未明顯
反映於其他消費項目通膨。惟考量能源成本傳導存在時間差，若油價維持高檔或
進一步上漲，未來通膨仍有進一步上升的風險。

用相關內容。

8

五、印度及東南亞—油價上揚、通膨升溫，進口成本上漲恐使貿易收支惡化 
東南亞:(1)印尼：7 月出口額 262.2 億美元、年增 6.05%，非油氣產品出口年增
6.84%，礦物燃料、鋼鐵及電氣機械及設備出口增幅均逾 2 成，油氣產品出口
大幅下滑 14.58%；對美國（8.61%）、中國（17.52%）、日本（7.93%）、東
協（16.72%） 出口強勁成長。7 月進口年增 27.02%達 260.9 億美元，創歷史新
高，非油氣產品及油氣產品進口分別年增 23.83%、49.91%，以機械設備、電
機及電氣設備及塑膠品進口成長為主 ， 自中國、 歐盟進口成長23.49%、26.32%、
自美進口成長 13.1%。進出口相抵，7 月貿易收支意外轉為順差 1.3 億美元。
印尼經濟較依賴進口 ， 部分進口需求係配合國內投資及生產活動擴張 ，另國際
油價上漲增加能源進口負擔，在出口成長幅度相對有限情形下 ， 進口持續快速
增加將壓縮商品貿易順差，並可能進一步擴大經常帳赤字。8 月消費者物價指
數(CPI)年增率升至 3.19%，主要因食品、飲料和香菸價格上漲，以及個人照護
和交通成本增加所致 ，剔除波動較大的食品和政府管制價格的核心通膨率升至
2.92%，為 2023 年 3 月以來最高。儘管通膨有所回升，但仍維持在印尼央行
1.5%至 3.5%的目標區間內。(2)泰國：8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率上升至
2.53%，主要因中東衝突導致油價上漲，進而推高國內燃料價格所致，食品、
非食品價格通膨分別上升 2.99%、2.23%，剔除能源及生鮮食品的核心通膨率
上揚至 1.44%，創 3 年多新高，泰國商務部維持其 2026 年整體通膨預期在
1.5%-2.5%之間，通膨水準仍處於泰國央行 1%-3%的目標區間內。(3)菲律賓：
8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率從 7 月的 6.2%降至 6.1%， 主要因食品/非酒精
飲料、房屋/公用事業價格漲幅放緩，惟受燃油價格上漲影響，交通運輸價格
進一步上揚 13.5%，核心通膨率小幅降至 4.1%，1 月至 8 月平均通膨率為 5.2%，
仍高於菲律賓央行 2%-4%的目標區間，在國際油價大幅上揚下，央行升息壓
力漸增。(4)越南: 8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增率上升至 4.89%，創 3 個月新
高，主要是運輸成本上升所致，儘管高於越南國家銀行(SBV)4.5%目標，惟核
心通膨穩定於 4.5%， 在經濟動能仍佳下 ， 央行尚有餘裕採取審慎觀望的政策。
8 月出口額年增 26%至 548 億美元，其中電子產品出口成長 41%為主要驅動
力，機械設備成長 34%居次，對美國出口大增 24.7%；8 月進口額年增 37.9%
至 549.1 億美元，以電子產品、電腦及零件成長 80%為最，機械設備居次。進
出口相抵，8 月貿易逆差收窄至 11 億美元。整體而言，越南受惠於人工智慧
需求及中國供應鏈移轉，帶動電子產品出口，但主要集中於外資企業，資本財

與生產投入高度仰賴進口， 對國內生產活動增值十分有限 ，越南政府正積極推
動投資技術轉移、提升治理能力及開拓市場，加強本土企業之間的聯繫，以創
造更大的附加價值。 
五、台灣—通膨略降溫，AI 需求支撐出口成長 
8 月消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.04%，雖較前兩月回落，但已連續 4 個月高
於 2%的通膨警戒線，主要受國際油價上漲帶動油料費、機票及旅遊團費增加，
加上外食費、房租及電費等價格上漲所致；惟蔬菜價格因去年基期較高而大幅下
跌，抵銷部分漲幅。整體而言，通膨壓力雖略有降溫，但核心 CPI 仍達 2.30%，
顯示外食、房租等服務類價格仍具黏著性，物價上漲壓力尚未完全消退。 
 8 月出口達 824 億美元，年增 41%，續創歷年單月新高，主要受惠於人工智
慧應用快速擴展及國際雲端服務業者持續加大資本支出，帶動半導體、伺服器及
相關資通訊產品需求強勁。雖然部分傳統產業仍受全球景氣復甦步調不一及貿易
環境不確定性影響，但科技產品需求熱絡使整體出口表現優於預期。展望未來，
在 AI、 高效能運算及雲端服務需求持續成長 ， 以及第四季傳統外銷旺季來臨帶動
下，我國出口動能可望延續，惟仍須關注地緣政治風險及國際貿易政策變化對外
需之影響。

市場焦點
美國
公債回購
• 美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，低於部分市場期待
的80億至100億美元規模 。民間持有美債規模達 31.8兆美元，相較之下 ，本次60億美元回購
僅約占民間持債規模 0.02%，對整體影響有限 。此外，約6成的美債期限不足 5年，意味未來
再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。
日本央行
• 日本第二季GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給
經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日本央行(BOJ)
逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶
值的政治壓力，彭博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。

美國
通膨
• 美國8月生產者物價指數 （PPI）年增5.4%，略高市場預期的 5.3%，核心PPI年增4.6%，符合
預期。8月消費者物價指數（CPI）年增3.35%、核心CPI年增2.4%，均符合市場預期，但核心
CPI月升幅0.3%，高於市場預期的 0.2%。數據公佈後，FedWatch顯示市場預期9月升息機率，
從前一周的50%攀升至86%。

美國8月通膨數據(CPI)
• 美國8月通膨數據：8月美國CPI月增0.4%、年增3.4%，核心CPI月增0.3%，核心服務通膨升溫至0.5%，顯示通膨降溫仍不
均衡，能源價格上漲為主要推手，市場預估8月核心PCE月增約0.3%、年增約3.8%，仍高於Fed 2%目標，搭配強勁就業數
據，9月升息1碼的壓力升高。不過，本次通膨主要來自能源、需求及AI投資帶動，與2021～2022年供給型通膨有所不同。
• 市場反應與展望：CPI公布後，2年期美債殖利率一度上升，惟長天期殖利率反而走低，顯示市場關注焦點並非單純升息，而
是Fed能否維持抗通膨政策可信度。整體而言，升息未必等同Risk-off，若經濟與企業獲利維持韌性，股市仍具支撐。
資料來源： investing.com，2026.9.14

能源成本回升推升PPI，通膨壓力牽動Fed政策空間
• 能源成本回升，PPI重新升溫：美國8月PPI月增0.4%、年增5.4%，增幅均明顯高於7月，主要受到商品價格上漲帶動；其
中最終需求商品價格月增1.1%，能源價格上升4.2%，柴油更是大漲逾2成，成為商品價格上升的重要來源，惟最終需求服
務價格僅略增0.1%，顯示本次PPI升溫仍以能源及商品價格為主。
• 成本壓力仍在，利率政策不確定性升高： 扣除食品、能源及貿易服務後的核心 PPI月增0.3%，微低於7月的0.4%，但年增
4.7%，顯示價格壓力仍未完全消退；其中運輸與倉儲服務價格上升2.3%，反映部分成本仍具黏著性，致使市場對Fed利率
政策的重新定價明顯升溫，進而推動股、債市短線波動擴大。
• 市場觀察：由於本次PPI升溫主要來自能源成本 ，但服務價格壓力整體仍相對溫和 ，因此尚不足以視為通膨全面升溫 ，然

能源價格若持續走高 ，將提高Fed維持較高利率的壓力 ，建議投資人短線宜降低長天期債券與高估值成長股的追價 ，債券
可優先配置短中天期、高品質債券，股票則可關注能源、金融及具定價能力企業，提高投組對利率與成本波動的承受度。
資料來源：U.S. Bureau of Labor Statistics，Producer Price Indexes – Aug,  2026，2026.9.11

• 財政部對美債的回購規模不如預期：美國財政部9月9日宣布，將於翌日回購最高60億美元的10至20年期美債，主要鎖定流

動性較低的舊券，以改善長天期債券市場流動性。雖然本次回購高於先前設定的每次至少40億美元，但低於部分市場期待的
80億至100億美元規模。消息公布後10年期公債殖利率逼近5%，30年期殖利率亦突破5.3%。
• 回購規模在高額赤字背景下，難以有效拉低長天期公債殖利率：美國今年公債發行規模較2025年增加11.8%，民間持有美債
規模達31.8兆美元、年增8.2%，相較之下，本次60億美元回購僅約占民間持債規模0.02%，對整體影響有限。此外，約6成
的美債期限不足5年，意味未來再融資需求仍高，若財政赤字未明顯收斂，長端殖利率上行壓力仍存。反觀新興國家基本面
持續改善，且當地債市有貨幣潛在升值空間，可作為全球債券配置的分散來源。

• 地緣政治動盪推升油價，但全球經濟依然展現韌性：美國和伊朗的衝突已經進入第7個月，能源價格上升及地緣政治風險仍
對全球經濟與市場信心形成壓力，但AI投資熱潮持續推升科技需求，使全球經濟展現優於預期的韌性。其中，亞洲仍是全球
成長動能最強的區域之一，2026年亞洲（不含日本）經濟成長率預估達4.7%，明顯高於全球平均2.9%及成熟市場1.6%。在
內需與科技出口支撐下，新興亞洲基本面相對穩健，具備較佳的中長期配置條件。

• AI資本支出延續，台韓成為核心受惠市場：AI資本支出持續擴張，2026年全球雲端AI資本支出預估達7,960億美元，並帶動
AI半導體市場至2030年突破7,500億美元。亞洲掌握全球半導體供應鏈核心，台灣受惠於先進製程與先進封裝需求，韓國則
受惠於HBM記憶體需求，台韓可望成為AI基礎建設擴張的主要受惠市場；同時，中國與印度則提供較穩定的內需及經濟成
長支撐。2026年亞洲企業盈餘成長率預估達72.13%，顯著高於全球平均，建議新興亞洲以台韓科技股為主要成長配置，並
搭配中國、印度市場分散區域風險。

短債供給增加未引發融資緊張，短天期債券防禦價值提升
• 回購市場維持穩定，短債供給暫未造成壓力：美國回購市場今年以來維持平穩，儘管9月短債償付與企業繳稅可能帶來短
暫資金波動，但SOFR與IORB利差仍處相對正常區間 ，顯示目前準備金與短期流動性尚稱充裕 。此外SOFR/IORB差值對
短期美債淨發行增加的敏感度明顯低於2025年底，反映流動性環境偏寬鬆。
• 貨幣基金成長放緩，供給吸收能力受到限制：零售資金流入偏弱，使貨幣市場基金資產增速落後 ，2026年迄今僅增加約
325億美元，遠低於2025年同期的2,050億美元；即便Q4季節性回升，新增資金對短債供給的承接能力仍可能有限。
• 後市觀察：年底前短債淨發行規模預估約3,250億美元，後續需關注短債供給、企業繳稅及財政部長端公債回購對資金面

的影響。建議投資人配置上可提高 1～3年期美國國庫券或短天期美債 ETF比重，以取得票息收益並降低長端殖利率波動
對債券價格的影響。
資料來源：US Treasury，FRBNY ，Federal Reserve ，2026.9.9

風險偏好升溫與債券供給增加，推升期限溢價與長債殖利率
• 全球期限溢酬上升，長債面臨重新定價：全球債券殖利率同步走高，不僅反映各國政策利率預期，也受到全球期限溢酬上升
推動；投資人對持有長天期政府債券要求更高補償 ，顯示長債殖利率上行已具有跨市場的結構性因素 。Bloomberg
Economics的模型亦顯示，全球期限溢酬是近年G7長債殖利率走高的重要共同因素。
• 風險資產吸引資金，放大長債供需壓力：AI投資題材推升股票市場、信用利差仍相對偏窄，加上大宗商品走強，使股票、公
司債及商品與政府債券形成資金競爭；在政府融資需求持續增加的情況下 ，若債券需求未同步擴張，政府公債需提供更高殖
利率才能吸引資金。
• 後市觀察：長端利率已成為股債配置的重要變數 ，若殖利率因財政供給與風險偏好維持高檔 ，長天期債券及高估值股票均可

能承壓；建議以短中天期美債及高品質投資級公司債為核心 ，待長端殖利率充分反映期限溢酬後，再分批拉長債券存續期間。

日本央行升息箭在弦上，但缺乏連續升息條件
• GDP修正支持升息 ，但關鍵點在配合日本和美國政府遏制日圓疲軟： 日本第二季 GDP年化季增率上修至 1.4%(優於初值
1.1%)，儘管伊朗戰爭引發原油供應緊張給經濟帶來壓力，但GDP增速依舊高於0.7%潛在經濟成長率，雖然經濟表現支持日
本央行(BOJ)逐步實施貨幣政策正常化 ，但更關鍵的催化劑來自日美兩國協調干預匯市 ，遏制日圓極端貶值的政治壓力 ，彭
博經濟研究預期日本央行將在9月會議上加息25個基點。
• 缺乏連續大幅加息的支撐力道： (1)經濟衝擊風險：日圓升值效應已對經濟產生緊縮效果 ，彭博預估可能拖累實際 GDP成長
達0.3%。(2)財政負擔過重：若政策利率升至 1.75%以上，將大幅增加政府利息支出 ，擠壓高市內閣的財政刺激空間 。(3)企

業投資放緩：私人資本支出仍顯疲軟(季比下降0.9%)，顯示經濟復甦基礎尚未完全穩固。
• 小結：日本央行在政治、匯率與經濟基本面交集下 ，9月升息已成定局，但考量財政負擔與全球金融穩定後 ，其後續加息步
伐應偏向謹慎與循序漸進 ，密切關注9月日本央行會議後聲明 ，若央行強調循序漸進而非連續緊縮 ，市場波動相對可控；反
之，若釋放過度鷹派訊號，則需防範市場拋售風險。由於市場資金對日圓套利交易依賴度仍高，日圓任何短期內的急速升值，
都極易觸發全球風險資產的連鎖去槓桿效應。

中國8月通膨回升，惟需求端疲弱
• 8月份 CPI及PPI：消費者物 價指 數 (CPI)月增 0.4%(前月月減 0.1%)、年增 0.8%(前月 0.5%)，生 產者物價指數 (PPI) 月增
0.4%(前月月減0.7%)、年增3.8%(前月3.5%)。通膨走揚主要受到能源及通訊設備成本上升影響。
• 觀點：中國自2023年以來，內需市場面臨房地產崩盤 、青年失業率飆升以及公共債務攀升等困境 。今年年初以來，通膨雖
然由負轉正，不過從細項得知，因國際油價飆升、AI硬體設備面臨漲價壓力，本波通膨回升主要是受到供給端影響。外部成
本壓力恐進一步壓抑中國內需消費，中國要在真正意義上走出通縮環境可能還需要更多官方政策發力。彭博預估，在需求未
出現明顯改善的情境下，中國央行需要近一步放寬貨幣政策，預測年底前可能降息10個基點。

資料來源：Bloomberg，截至2026.9.9。

AI產業獲利亮眼，但需留意變現能力和資本效率
• AI獲利增速領先大盤，銷售成長成為獲利關鍵：AI板塊在2026年第一與第二季分別實現52.8%與59.7%的高獲利成長，受高
基期影響，未來四季度平均獲利成長預計放緩至 34.4%，但仍大幅優於非 AI板塊12.3%。預計2026年第三季至2027年第二
季，AI平均銷售成長率將達 27.2%，為非AI成分股(5%)5倍以上 。隨著未來利潤率擴張放緩 (預計未來 6個季度平均僅提升
1.66%，低於過去12個季度的3.52%)，持續的銷售成長是維持獲利的關鍵。
• AI供應鏈內部發展並不均衡： 半導體最為強勁，2026Q3至2027Q2預期銷售成長65.8%，獲利大增112.1%且淨利潤率大幅
擴張8.86%，為產業鏈核心引擎。雲端巨頭表現穩健，營收與獲利增速維持約26%，基礎設施軟體與硬體也有15%~20%營

收與獲利成長 。但應用端軟體成長相對乏力 ，未來四季度獲利預期增速僅 4.3%與6.5%，表現低於標普500非AI股平均水準。
• 資本強度創新高 ，但自由現金流承壓： AI資本支出佔營收比重自 2024年Q1的8.9%一路攀升至 2026年Q2的20.1%，預計
2026年下半年將達到23%的高點。資本支出激增導致自由現金流與資本支出之比(FCF/Capex)從2.48倍滑落至0.69倍，自由
現金流利潤率亦從21%降至13.9%。隨著現金逐漸消耗，企業可能需透過債券市場進行發債融資 ，以維持龐大基礎設施投資。
• 小結與投資建議：AI板塊依然是美股獲利驅動力，但隨利潤率提升空間受限、自由現金流受資本支出壓制，投資邏輯已從全
面暴漲轉向注重變現能力與資本效率。投資建議聚焦上游硬體與半導體，建議持續優選具備高定價能力與實質訂單支撐的半
導體與雲端基礎設施龍頭，留意資本支出佔比過高且自由現金流急劇惡化公司，優先選擇現金流充沛、融資成本低企業。
標普500指數盈利成長：AI vs. 非AI

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
Hermes Testing Solutions
Shifting-left, scaling-up: dual catalysts in chip
probing and CPO
Jack-of-all-trades for bespoke testing solutions in the semiconductor industry
We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 PT. HTSI is
uniquely positioned to benefit from two key semiconductor testing trends: shift-left
testing and the emerging CPO testing market. Leveraging its long-standing relationship

with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across electrical test engineering,
customised test equipment, thermal control modules, probe cards, and CPO testing
solutions. We forecast revenue growth of 127%/152%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, driven
by increasing adoption of thermal control modules for wafer-level test and initial
deployment of CPO insertion 2 and insertion 3 test equipment through ficonTEC.
Additional 2028E growth drivers include reflow tools, laser clean solutions, and MEMS

probe cards. The stock trades at 27/15x 2027/28E PE, versus ~35x NTM PE for handler-
equipment peers, offering re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified,
and its CPO presence expands. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Shift-left beneficiary with exceptional thermal control modules
Thermal control modules should be HTSI's largest growth driver over the next several
years. We expect increasing chip power consumption, rising package complexity, and

tighter probe pitches to drive adoption of advanced thermal control solutions used
alongside leading-edge probers, in additional to more wafer level insertions and longer
test time. We estimate HTSI will ship approximately 3,000 thermal-control modules in
2027E, up from around 1,200 units in 2026E, sales contribution rise from ~30% in
Q226 to >50% of sales by 2027E. The launch of its second-generation platform in
2027E, which carries 25-50% higher ASPs, should further improve profitability. We

forecast GM expansion from 55.5% in 2026E to 58.3%/60.0% in 2027E/28E.
Unique position to capture CPO upside
Through its partnership with German optical-automation leader ficonTEC, HTSI is
positioned to participate in the initial ramp of CPO testing equipment. HTSI supports
local manufacturing, installation, automation and after-sales service, making it a key
enabler of volume production. We estimate HTSI captures approximately 30% of
equipment value across the 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems we forecast for

delivery in 2027E, with further potential upside in 2028E as adoption expands.
Valuation: initiate coverage with a Buy rating and price target NT$8,880
We forecast EPS of NT$29.1 in 2026E (including a one-off employee share purchase
programme impact), rising to NT$153.3/286.5 in 2027E/28E. Our NT$8,880 price target
is based on 31x average 2028E PE, versus handler-equipment peers forward PE at
roughly 35x, reflecting HTSI's unique exposure to both shift-left testing, CPO, and our

estimate of 32% LT earnings CAGR. We initiate with a Buy rating.

### Page 2

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 2
Hermes Testing Solutions UBS Research
Thesis Map     UBS                    Research     THESIS MAP a guide to our thinking and what´s where in this report
Q: Will HTSI be able to keep its moat within chip probing thermal control modules?
Yes. We expect HTSI's competitive position to strengthen as chip power consumption, package
complexity, and probe density continue to increase. The company is developing next-generation

thermal control solutions, including liquid cooling technologies, while also expanding into specialized
chuck solutions designed to address wafer warpage. In addition, HTSI's close collaboration with
TSMC, TEL, Advantest, and other OSATs should reinforce its position within advanced wafer-level
testing.
Q: Will insertion 2 of CPO testing process be skipped?
No. We believe concerns surrounding insertion 2 adoption are overdone. While some industry

participants favor bypassing insertion 2 due to throughput and ROI considerations, doing so increases
the risk of defective optical engines being identified only after the components are delivered to
OSATs, creating yield accountability challenges and higher packaging losses. Based on our industry
studies, we expect insertion 2 testing to remain an important part of the CPO workflow over at least
the next two years as the industry focuses on improving PIC yields and overall testing efficiency.

We initiate coverage of Hermes Testing (HTSI) with a Buy rating and NT$8,880 price target. HTSI
offers unique exposure to two of the semiconductor testing industry's most attractive growth drivers:
1) shift-left testing and 2) CPO testing. The industry's shift-left testing strategy will have more test
insertions move to the wafer level before packaging, in addition to longer CP time. Leveraging its
longstanding relationship with TSMC, HTSI has established capabilities across thermal control

modules, electrical-test engineering services, customized equipment, and emerging CPO testing
solutions. We believe there could be re-rating potential as sales scale, new products are qualified, and
its CPO presence expands.
TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'

pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand.
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI currently trades at just 15x of our 2028E EPS versus
handler-equipment peers forward PE at roughly 35x. We believe market expectations for 2027 CPO
testing equipment shipments remain conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on
our industry analysis, we estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E,

this should be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3 shipment
due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO adoption expands. Our
reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR
vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for
re-rating.

Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It focuses on providing

semiconductor testing and inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical support, and
maintenance services. Its main business segments are customized semiconductor equipment,
engineering services, and probe cards and other materials.

### Page 3

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 3
Hermes Testing Solutions UBS Research
Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 3S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l

We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and

card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and

Nonetheless, leaders within the testing sector will standout among competition, with

We see HTSI's sales for the next few years to mainly be driven by 1) thermal control
module shipment and 2) CPO equipment shipment. In the past, thermal control

modules weren't necessary for chip probing, due to less TDP for the chips. However, as
new iterations of AI accelerators now got substantial power draw, this unlocks a brand
new TAM for HTSI to enter with their thermal control modules to pair with probers, in
order to prevent probe card damages.
 Robust thermal control module shipments.  We estimate HTSI to ship
3,000/4,100 thermal control modules in 2027E/28E, with continuous demand in
future years, bringing their 2026-31E semiconductor equipment sales CAGR to

48%.

from Advantest's announced capacity expansion plan (testers will be paired with

Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe HTSI is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers and
capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see HTSI is uniquely positioned as
the main beneficiary of two of the strongest growth drivers within semiconductor
testing: testing shift-left and CPO.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing

### Page 5

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 5
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging.
Figure 4: CP is set to grow substantially with shift-left, each packaging step set to be MORE costly than the prior step

RDL / RWAT testing Wafer-level CP (WLCP)
CoWChiplet (if use SoIC) CoW
CP (Die A)
CP (Die B)
Assembly Test (AST)
oS
Source: UBS
The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while
defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed

testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million
(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall

integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of
wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level

chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

Hermes Testing (HTSI) is a key beneficiary of this trend. Leveraging its long-standing
relationship with TSMC and expertise in semiconductor test solutions, HTSI supplies

advanced thermal control modules used alongside leading-edge probers (made by
Tokyo Electron), particularly for AI applications. These thermal control modules are
required for high TDP semiconductor wafer probe in order to maintain the temperature

### Page 6

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 6
of the chuck to avoid needle burns or probe card damages. We estimate HTSI will ship
approximately 3,000 thermal-management modules in 2027E, up from 1,200 in 2026E,
supporting sales growth of 127%/152% in 2026E/27E. HTSI is also the partner for
prober installation, prober maintenance and services. In addition, HTSI provides high-
end chuck processing services, an area that should benefit from increasing package sizes

and higher wafer warpage associated with advanced packaging.
Beyond thermal control modules, HTSI also supplies consumables and customised
solutions for wafer-level testing, including probe card cleaning sheets and laser-based
probe head cleaning systems. As probing intensity increases under the shift-left testing
model, demand for cleaning and maintenance solutions should rise alongside probe
card utilisation. Currently, cleaning sheets is around 5% of sales, but we believe it could

gain traction in 2027/28E and beyond. We see additional upside from future modular
laser cleaning systems that could be integrated directly with probers, further expanding
HTSI's content opportunity within advanced wafer-level testing.
CPO testing fuelling future growth for semiconductor testing
As interconnect bottlenecks increasingly shift from the memory wall to the copper wall,
we believe co-packaged optics (CPO) adoption is inevitable over the longer term. Testing

remains one of the key bottlenecks to CPO deployment, as optical validation differs
fundamentally from the electrical testing processes that have dominated semiconductor
manufacturing for decades. As a result, CPO is creating a new testing opportunity across
the semiconductor equipment and consumables ecosystem. We have written
extensively on CPO and opportunities that it brings to the semiconductor supply chain,
see our CPO deep dive , our thoughts on CPO timing , 2026 SiPhIA conference

takeaways, and SEMICON 2026 takeaways.
Various test equipment suppliers are scrambling to come up with ideal testing
equipment across the four insertions of CPO testing, with solutions to ensure both
optical and electrical signal integrity of the CPO solutions. The four insertions are:
 Insertion 1: Electronic IC (EIC)/Photonic IC (PIC) test.  Single-side wafer test
that has dedicated probe cards to test the fabricated EIC/PIC wafers.
 Insertion 2: Optical/Electrical test. Double sided wafer test, testing the hybrid

bonded (e.g. via TSMC's COUPE) EIC and PIC wafers by an opto-electrical test that
verifies the electrical signals on the top and optical signal on the bottom of the
hybrid bonded wafers.
 Insertion 3: Optical engine (OE) test.  The OE tests are done to ensure the
quality of the OEs and with FAU (Fiber Array Unit) attached, to ensure the signal
integrity of the OEs. The testing equipment needs to be able to handle 224G per
channel data rates, requiring high unit per hour (UPH) throughput, meticulous dirt

mitigation, and maintaining a near 100% contact rating on high-speed sockets.
 Insertion 4: ASIC final test.  This final test step is established post the OEs are
packaged together with the ASIC. The complete ASIC package (now with CPO) is
now tested for opto-electrical functionalities.
As it stands, insertion 2 and insertion 3 are the two areas where there are the most
debates in the industry. Insertion 3 suppliers such as Chroma has noted the trend of

“shift-to-middle” paradigm centered on insertion 3. In high-density scale-out switches
deploying up to 32 optical engines (e.g. Nvidia’s Spectrum-X architectures), any OE
defect results in package scrap at the CoWoS level, making 100% known good die
(KGD) validation at the singulated OE stage an absolute commercial requirement.

With its 25 years of precision test equipment expertise, ficonTEC is an early mover for
CPO test equipment and has key know-how for optical alignment, which is
quintessential for optical test. HTSI, through its partnership with ficonTEC, is set to be a
major beneficiary once CPO proliferates in 2027E-28E. As the partner of ficonTEC,
Hermes Testing is the key enabler that can help ficonTEC achieve HVM for its CPO

testing equipment. As of now, our view is that both insertion 2 and insertion 3 should be
required within the next two years, despite the difficulties surrounding double-sided
wafer probing (insertion 2), as the yield for OEs are still not high enough to avoid
defective dies. Finding out known-good OEs will be crucial before the FAU coupling
process during the latter stages of CPO packaging, to prevent impeding the final
package with multiple OEs attached to the switch ASIC (e.g. 32 OEs on Nvidia's

Spectrum-X). We estimate Hermes Testing will ship 40/70 units of insertion 2/3 testing
equipment in 2027E, with potential upside to the numbers as we view Hermes Testing
will likely assist with the ramp up for Gen 2 insertion 3 equipment in 2027E. Gen 2
insertion equipment will come with better throughput and ASP .

Beyond insertions 2 and 3, HTSI is also positioned to participate in insertion 1 testing
through customised prober solutions for photonic integrated circuit (PIC) testing. We
believe these solutions could eventually be packaged as licensable intellectual property,

creating an additional long-term growth opportunity as CPO adoption broadens. While
near-term testing growth is driven by AI accelerators, CPO represents the next major
expansion cycle for semiconductor testing. We believe HTSI is uniquely positioned to
participate across multiple test insertions, providing exposure to a potentially significant
source of incremental revenue beginning in 2027E.

HTSI's unique position for new TAM of thermal control
modules and CPO test equipment
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan, as a spin-off
from Hermes-Epitek. The company provides semiconductor testing and inspection

solutions, engineering services, customized equipment, and test consumables. HTSI
maintains a global service footprint across Taiwan (Hsinchu, Tainan), Mainland China
(Suzhou), Japan (Tokyo, Kumamoto), Singapore, Malaysia, and the United States
(Arizona), enabling close support for leading semiconductor customers.
Its main business segments are structured around three pillars:
 Semiconductor equipment and customized products : HTSI utilises a dual-

track strategy of independent R&D and also equipment agency sales. They offers

### Page 9

Hermes Testing Solutions 8 October 2026 ab 9
highly customized solutions designed to address specific client pain points and
bottlenecks. Key products include: thermal control modules that pair with a 1:1
ratio with wafer-level test probers, electron beam inspection equipment, white
light interference optical inspection systems, laser probe cleaning machines, and
the SYMPHONY fluxless reflow system.
 Testing equipment engineering services:  The company operates the largest

engineering service team in Taiwan, consisting of over 340 technical professionals.
The engineering service team provides extensive equipment life-cycle support,
including installation, relocations, modification, testing, routine maintenance, and
repair services. CPO related equipment sales/services will be recognized within this
segment, as CPO are not direct HTSI products, but rather sales recognition via its
partnership with ficonTEC.
 Probe card design, manufacturing, and cleaning materials:  HTSI delivers a

comprehensive product portfolio that supports the entire probe card life-cycle. For
probe cards, they supply membrane, cantilever, and vertical probe cards. As they
are small in scale, HTSI targets niche probe card applications such as RF, 5G/6G,
millimeter wave, LCD driver, low end logics, TIA (Transimpedance Amplifier), etc. In
addition to the three existing probe technologies, HTSI also collaborates with MJC
in Japan for MEMS probe development, targeting sales in 2028E for high-end, AI/

HPC related applications. Cleaning materials is another important business within
this segment, as HTSI source and process sand paper or other materials to offer
cleaning sheets to clean the probe cards, removing oxidations, unwanted particles
and substances that accumulated on the probe cards during the testing process.
The sales resiliency in the coming years mainly stems from its robust relationship with key
industry players, especially as an engineering and service provider with TSMC for testing

solutions. This sets up a solid foundation for its customized equipment business, as they
know the specific testing bottleneck to tackle. For instance, as Hermes-Epitek
manufacturers the probers for Tokyo Electron (TEL) and HTSI helps with installation,
services, and maintenance of the probers, they understand the thermal bottlenecks that
the wafer-level test industry has been facing and can effectively come up with module
solutions to solve this.

Margins set to expand with value-added products and services
The company has demonstrated strong execution in advanced wafer-level testing, with
H126 revenue of NT$2.2bn already exceeding 90% of FY25 revenue (NT$2.4bn).
Profitability has also improved significantly, with GM increasing from 42.1% in 2025 to
54.9% in H126.
With share of value-added products and services set to rise in 2027E in sales mix, HTSI is
set to see GM and operating leverage expansion. As a niche engineering service provider

that has robust ties to leading foundries and OSATs, we believe HTSI has substantial
pricing power and estimate HTSI' GM for thermal control modules to be around 65%,
with GM for CPO equipment of around 60% as HTSI provides an engineering-service-
like business for it. We believe these factors will help the company to reach a 60% long-
term GM range.
OPM is also set to expand with customized equipment sales rise in total sales mix. If we
exclude the one-time expense to be recognized during Q326 for employee stock

benefits (we estimate around NT$1bn), YoY opex growth will be around 99% for
2027E, with downside risk if the company conducts tight cost control measures. We
forecast the company's OPM will expand from 19.5% in 2026E, to 44.3%/47.0% in
2027/28E.

Source: LSEG, UBS
Despite a 264% YTD share-price increase, HTSI still trades at just 15x 2028E P/E. We
believe market expectations for 2027E CPO testing equipment shipments remain
conservative, particularly for insertion 2 equipment. Based on our industry analysis, we
estimate HTSI will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, this should
be higher than the market's expectation of no insertion 2 and limited insertion 3

shipment due to engineering bottleneck. We also see further upside in 2028E as CPO
adoption expands. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 17% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 27% and long-term GM
level of 33% vs our estimate of 61%. We see scope for re-rating.
We believe the market is not fully reflecting three key growth drivers:
1) Shift-left testing is still underappreciated.
As advanced packaging costs rise and AI accelerators become increasingly complex,

more thorough testing is done at the wafer-level. This should drive sustained demand
for HTSI's thermal control modules, probe card consumables, and other wafer-level
testing solutions.
2) CPO revenue potential remains underestimated.
Investor expectations for 2027 CPO equipment shipments, particularly insertion 2
testing systems, remain conservative. Based on our industry studies, we forecast HTSI
will deliver 40 insertion 2 and 70 insertion 3 systems in 2027E, with further potential

upside in 2028E as CPO adoption broadens.
3) New products provide additional optionality.
Beyond its core businesses, HTSI is expanding into higher-value solutions including mass-
reflow tools, MEMS probe cards and laser cleaning systems, creating incremental
growth opportunities that we believe are largely absent from current expectations.
We value HTSI using 31x average 2028E PE, underpinned by a 32% 2027-31E earnings
CAGR. We use 2028E PE as our view is that 2028 is the year for thermal control modules

and CPO adoption grow from the initial ramping up stage to mass adoption stage. We
believe using handlers as a peer group is justifiable for a unique company like HTSI, with
thermal control modules for chip probing sharing similar characteristics as active thermal
control used for final test, in addition to Hon Precision and Chroma having exposure to
CPO test equipment. For equipment vendors, the peer group's average NTM PE post
2022 is around 35x, and the group is currently trading at around 41x NTM PE (see

What's Priced In chart).
HTSI is trading at NT$4,190 as of 6
Oct 2026

Source: UBS estimates
Risk to the current share price is heavily skewed (2.2:1) to the upside.
UPSIDE (NT$10,000): Our upside scenario reflects stronger than expected thermal
module and CPO equipment shipment in 2027-28E, with additional upside from better
traction of probe cards and mass reflow tools, forming 157%/91% YoY sales growth in
2027/28E and even higher operating leverage with OPM of 46.3%/50.0% in 2027/28E.

This scenario implies a 34x 2028E PE, a upside driving by more equipment demand
stemming for robust shift-left test induced equipment bottleneck, alongside CPO
proliferation with scale-up CPO equipment pull-in in 2028E.
BASE (NT$8,880): Our price target of NT$8,880 is based on 31x 2028E PE. We expect an
earnings CAGR of 32% in 2027-31E. With shift-left trend for more thermal module
requirement during testing, alongside 40/70 CPO insertion 2/3 shipment. We see these

growth drivers driving YoY sales growth from a high base of 127% in 2026E to
152%/78% YoY in 2027/2028E, with OPM of 44.3%/47.0% in 2027/28E.
DOWNSIDE (NT$1,500): Our downside scenario reflects lower shipment of thermal
control modules due to less advanced prober demand with less insertion 2 and 3
equipment shipment due to bottlenecks with CPO production, alongside limited
additional growth drivers to be picked up in 2028 such as MEMS probe card and mass

reflow tools. This leads to weaker YoY sales growth of 67%/50% in 2027/28E and
operating margin of 29.3%/32.0% in 2027/28E as equipment shipment fails to scale.
This scenario implies a 14x 2028E PE, as valuation de-rate with dimmed outlook without
CPO and limited upside from thermal control module shipments.

Company Description
Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu,
Taiwan. It focuses on providing semiconductor testing and
inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical support,
and maintenance services. Its main business segments are
customized semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and
probe cards and other materials.

Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC
applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

Company Description
Hermes Testing Solutions (HTSI) was founded in 2004 in Hsinchu, Taiwan. It focuses on
providing semiconductor testing and inspecting solutions, equipment installation, technical
support, and maintenance services. Its main business segments are customized

semiconductor equipment, engineering services, and probe cards and other materials.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Hermes Testing based on PE methodology.
Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less

investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected front-end/CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in
Hermes Testing gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.

- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
Hermes Testing losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
Hermes Testing, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.

- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact Hermes
Testing’s margins for equipment shipments.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー

2026 年 10 月 9 日 全8 頁

AI 脅威論にどう向き合うべきか 
安全確保と開発競争の両立に向けた企業間協調の可能性と限界

AI アナリティックリサーチ室  主任研究員 田邉 美穂 
  主任研究員 新田 尭之

[要約] 
 最先端AI の急速な能力向上に伴い、AI がもたらす脅威への警戒が再び強まっている。
2026 年 9 月、Anthropic のダリオ・アモデイ CEO は、安全性の検証や対策が追いつく
範囲に開発速度を抑える必要性を訴え、企業間協調を提案した。企業や政府の間では、
開発ペースの調整や国際的な統制をめぐって立場が分かれる一方、能力評価やインシ
デント報告などでは協調が模索されており、9 月29 日には米国のAI 関連企業6 社が、
ホワイトハウスで安全確保に関する自主的な共同コミットメントに署名した。 
 AI 脅威論には、AI の高度化そのものがもたらす脅威と、AI の開発・保有・普及をめぐ
る脅威という二つの側面がある。前者には、人間による制御の喪失やサイバー攻撃な
どへの悪用があり、後者には、高度なAI 能力の一部の企業・国家への集中や、利用で
きる主体とできない主体間の格差拡大がある。前者に備えて単独で開発を減速すれば
他者に優位を奪われ、危険な能力へのアクセスを制限すれば後者の集中や格差を強め
かねない。安全確保と開発競争の間には、こうした二つのジレンマがある。 
 これらへの対応には企業や国家による協調が必要だが、その難しさは企業間と国家間
で異なる。企業間では他社も同等の安全対策を実施すると確認できれば、能力評価や
情報共有などで協調の余地がある。ただし、協調が機能するには①主要企業の参加、
②検証の信頼性、③副作用の抑制が求められるが、企業間の合意だけではこれらを満
たしにくい。さらに国家間では相手国の減速を確認しにくく、開発を続けることが合
理的になりやすい。このため企業間協調だけでは限界があり、企業間の安全基準や評
価方法を各国の制度に取り込み、各国の言語や利用環境における実効性を独立した立
場から検証できる主体を増やす必要がある。 
 日本に期待されるのは、こうした検証を担い、企業間で形成された安全基準を国際的
な枠組みへ接続する橋渡し役である。日本は広島AI プロセスを通じて国際指針や行動
規範の形成を主導してきたものの、AI セーフティ・インスティテュート（AISI）の本
格的な評価・検証体制の整備はこれからである。AI の能力向上と企業間協調をめぐる
議論が急速に進む中、最先端AI の能力・リスクに関する評価結果や安全対策の実効性
を自ら技術的に検証できる体制を、早急に整えることが求められる。

### Page 2

1

2 / 8

1. AI 開発の減速をめぐって割れる企業と政府 
アモデイ氏の問題提起 
2026 年9 月、Anthropic のダリオ・アモデイCEO は、最先端AI の能力向上が速すぎれば、安
全性の検証や対策が追いつかなくなるとして、主要企業が歩調を合わせて開発ペースを落とす
必要性を訴えた1。背景には、AI が次世代 AI の開発を加速させる再帰的自己改善の進展と、評
価中のAI エージェントが外部システムに侵入する事案が複数の企業で生じたことなどがある。
アモデイ氏は、人間による制御の喪失に加え、サイバー攻撃やバイオテロへの悪用、深刻な経
済的混乱などを主な脅威として挙げている。 
もっとも、アモデイ氏の主張は、AI の開発自体の停止を求めるものではない。むしろ、安全
性の検証に必要な時間を確保しながら開発を進めるため、①開発企業が外部評価者を受け入れ
る、②民主主義国のフロンティアAI 企業が政府の仲介や米国の独占禁止法（反トラスト法）の
限定的な適用除外の下で協調する、③国家間の協調へ広げる、という協調のあり方を提案して
いる。ただし、開発ペースの調整は米国が中国に対する技術的優位を維持できる範囲に限られ
るとし、対中輸出管理やモデル蒸留（他の AI モデルの回答を学習し、別の AI モデルの性能を
高める手法）への対策強化などを前提としている。

AI 企業各社と米国政府の反応 
この問題提起に対し、OpenAI やGoogle DeepMind などは企業間協調に理解を示した。また、
OpenAI のサム・アルトマン CEO は、開発速度を調整する必要性に同意し、独立した評価者に従
業員並みのアクセスを認めるというアモデイ氏の提案について、自社も同様に取り組む方針を
示した2。一方、Meta のマーク・ザッカーバーグCEO は、競争と法的責任が各社による安全確保
を促すとの考えを示し、NVIDIA のジェンスン・フアン CEO も AI のリスクを過度に強調する見
方に否定的な立場を取った3。ただし、Meta も第三者による独立評価には前向きであるほか 、
同社と NVIDIA はいずれも 9 月 29 日の共同コミットメント（後述）には署名している。各社の
立場は、安全確保か開発継続かで単純に二分できるものではない。 
こうした議論は企業間にとどまらず、国際安全保障上の問題へと広がった。9 月 23 日の国連
安全保障理事会では、アルトマン氏、アモデイ氏ら が AI と国際安全保障について意見を述べ
た。アルトマン氏は、人間が AI を制御できなくなることと、強力なAI が少数の企業・国家に
集中することを主要なリスクに挙げ、AI の能力とリスクの評価、人間による監督の維持、イン
シデント報告などに関する国際的な共通基準を求めた4。

1 Dario Amodei“We Must Pace the Frontier”, September 2026. 
2 SiliconANGLE“Sam Altman and Elon Musk back Dario Amodei's call to slow down the frontier of AI 
development”, September 13, 2026. 
3 日本経済新聞 電子版「アンソロピックCEO『競合批判やめる』 AI 暴走脅威に業界結束訴え」（2026 年9
月 16 日） 
4 OpenAI“Sam Altman’s remarks at the United Nations Security Council”, September 23, 2026.

### Page 3

1

3 / 8

これに対し、同じ会合で米国政府は、AI をめぐる国際対話には関与するものの、それが国際
的な統制の枠組みへ発展することには否定的な立場を示した。国際的な枠組みによって米国の
AI 開発が制約されることには否定的である5。 
もっとも、米国政府は、AI のリスクに対する限定的な協力や管理まで否定しているわけでは
ない。翌 24 日の米中首脳会談では、AI のリスクと便益について意見を交換する枠組みと、AI
インシデントに対応する二国間連絡チャネルを設けることで合意した6。開発ペースの調整や共
通規制には踏み込まなかったものの、重大な事態に備えた連絡や情報共有など、両国の利害が
一致しやすい分野では限定的な協力が具体化している。米国内でも、最先端AI の能力評価や外
国への技術流出対策など、安全保障上の管理が進められており、議会には独立評価やインシデ
ント報告を求める法案が提出されている7。さらに9 月 29 日には、Anthropic、OpenAI、Google、
Meta、xAI、NVIDIA の 6 社の代表者 がホワイトハウスで “Joint Commitment on Frontier 
Responsibilities”に署名し、内部統制の強化、外部監査人との連携、基準づくりのための定
期協議を約束した。ただし、これは法的拘束力のない自主的な取り組みであり、トランプ政権
は政府による規制よりも自主規制を重視する姿勢を改めて示した8。 
このように、AI の安全性をめぐっては、企業間・国家間で共通の対応を模索する動きが見ら
れる一方、開発ペースや規制のあり方をめぐる立場には隔たりがある（図表1）。

図表 1 AI 開発と安全確保をめぐる各主体の主な見解

（注）各主体の立場は一様ではなく、安全確保、規制、開発速度、国家間競争の各論点で見解が異なる。 
（出所）各社・各国政府・国連の公表資料、各種報道より大和総研作成

5 United Nations“Ongoing Efforts to Create Powerful AI a ‘Race Where Everyone Loses’, UN Expert 
Tells Security Council, as Members, Tech Leaders Debate Governance Issues”, September 23, 2026.

29, 2026. 
主な考え方主な主体立場
安全確保の時間を確保するため、
最先端AIの開発速度を調整し、
共通の評価基準などを検討
Anthropic、OpenAI、Googleなど企業間協調を重視
安全確保は各社の責任であり、
過度な規制や開発規制は避けるべきMeta、NVIDIAなど個社対応・市場競争を重視
他国に主導権を奪われるリスクをもつ
開発減速には慎重米国政府（特にトランプ政権）など国家間競争を重視

### Page 4

1

4 / 8

2. AI 脅威論の二つの側面と、安全確保と開発競争のジレンマ 
AI 脅威論①：AI の高度化そのものがもたらす脅威 
AI 脅威論の一つ目の捉え方は、AI の能力が高度化すること自体が、人間や社会に大きな被害
をもたらす可能性に注目するものである。代表的な論点として、AI が人間の意図とは異なる目
標を追求する問題や、人間がAI の判断や行動を十分に監視・制御できなくなる（制御喪失）リ
スクが挙げられる。AI が次世代 AI の開発を支援し、自身の能力向上を加速させる再帰的自己
改善が進めば、人間による評価や安全対策が追いつかなくなるとの懸念もある。もっとも、こ
うした懸念が実際に顕在化する時期や確率を見積もることは難しく、不確実性が大きい。 
一方、近年は、サイバー攻撃の支援や脆弱性の探索、生物学的な情報の生成など、AI が現時
点で持つ危険な能力を、具体的な評価や事案から把握する動きが進んでいる。2026 年夏には、
評価中の AI エージェントが外部システムに侵入する事案が複数の企業で相次いで明らかにな
り、評価環境の外部に影響を及ぼし得る能力が示された9。ただし、これを制御喪失の兆候とみ
るか、安全装置を外した評価環境における封じ込めの失敗とみるかで見方は分かれる。危険な
能力の確認が実社会での大規模な被害に直結するとは限らず、将来的な制御喪失のシナリオと、
現時点で観測される危険な能力を区別して評価する必要がある。

AI 脅威論②：AI の開発・保有・普及がもたらす脅威 
AI 脅威論の二つ目の捉え方は、AI そのものの危険性だけでなく、高度な AI を誰が開発・保
有し、誰が利用できるかが、企業や国家の力関係を変化させることに注目するものである。最
先端AI は、経済成長や情報収集、サイバー・軍事能力などに影響し得るため、その開発・保有
能力は国家安全保障上の優位にもつながり得る。このため、米中間では、AI モデルだけでなく、
半導体、計算資源、クラウド、人材などを含む開発基盤全体が競争の対象となっている。モデ
ル蒸留などをめぐる問題も、知的財産の問題にとどまらず、高度なAI 能力が競合企業や他国へ
移転する経済安全保障上の問題として捉えられている。 
危険な能力へのアクセス制限は、悪用や無制限な拡散を抑えるために必要となり得る一方、
最先端 AI を利用できる国・企業と利用できない主体との格差を広げる可能性がある。 実際、
2026 年 6 月には、米国政府がAnthropic に対して最先端モデルへの外国籍者のアクセスを全面
禁止する輸出規制措置を発出した結果、同社が対象モデルの提供を全世界で一時停止し、日本
からも一時的に利用できなくなった10。また、安全規制への対応に高度な評価能力や多額の費
用が必要になれば、既存大手への集中を強め、新規参入を妨げるおそれもある。このように、
高度なAI 能力が一部の国家・企業に集中することと、危険な能力が無制限に拡散することの双

9 Anthropic“Investigating three real-world incidents in our cybersecurity evaluations”, July 30, 
2026; OpenAI“The Hugging Face incident and the road ahead”, August 26, 2026; among others. 
10 新田尭之・田邉美穂「米国政府はなぜ最先端 AI を停止させたのか：最先端AI モデルへの輸出規制措置が示
す AI 統治の転換点」（大和総研レポート、2026 年6 月17 日）

### Page 5

1

5 / 8

方がリスクとなり、その能力を誰が保有し、誰が利用や移転を管理するのかが重要な論点とな
っている。

安全確保と開発競争のジレンマと協調を阻む構造 
これら二つの脅威は相互に影響している。AI の能力が高まるほど、安全対策の必要性と、そ
の能力を保有する経済的・安全保障上の価値がともに高まり、企業間・国家間の開発競争が激
しくなる。競争を優先すれば安全対策が後回しになりやすいが、単独で開発ペースを落とせば、
減速しない企業や国家が技術的な優位を得る可能性がある。このため、危険性を認識しても開
発競争から離脱しにくいというジレンマが生じる。 また、危険な能力へのアクセス制限や安全
規制は、悪用や拡散を抑える一方、能力の集中や利用格差 の拡大といった別のジレンマを生み
かねない（図表2）。 
こうした二つのジレンマへの対応には、企業や国家による協調が必要となる。ただし、AI で
は開発の主体と最先端の能力が国家ではなく民間企業に集中しているため、 協調は企業間と国
家間の二つの層で求められ、その難しさもそれぞれ異なる。 
企業間では、各社が安全対策の必要性を共有し、他社も同等の対策を実施すると確認できれ
ば、歩調を合わせる余地がある。相互の協力が期待できる状況では、協調が各社にとって最も
望ましく、抜け駆けする誘因は小さい。こうした安全対策の共通化は、協調の成否が相互の信
頼にかかるという点で、いわゆる「鹿狩りゲーム（全員が協力すれば最大の成果が得られるが、
他者の協力を信じられなければ各自が確実だが小さな成果を選んでしまう状況を表すゲーム理
論のモデル）」に近い構図である。実際、OpenAI、Anthropic、Google は、AI の安全性をめぐる
企業間協調について協議を進めている11。ただし、中国企業やオープンモデルとの競争も続い
ており、協調に加わらない主体が存在すれば、参加企業も 競争に備えざるを得ず、企業間協調
は不安定になる。 
国家間では、相手国が減速すれば抜け駆けして優位に立てる一方、相手国が開発を続ければ
減速した自国だけが後れを取る。このため、相手国の行動にかかわらず、自国は国内企業の開
発を後押しすることが合理的になりやすい。その結果、双方が減速する方が互いに望ましい場
合でも、双方が開発を続ける状態に陥りやすい。これは、いわゆる「囚人のジレンマ（各自が
自分にとって合理的な選択をした結果、全員にとって望ましくない結果に陥る状況を表すゲー
ム理論のモデル） 」に近い。合意によって減速を約束しても、各国には約束を破る誘因が残る
うえに、相手国が守っているかを確認することも難しい。国家間競争は自国企業に開発継続を
促すため、企業間の協調だけでは安全確保と開発競争のジレンマを解消できるとは限らない。

2026.

3. 企業間協調の可能性と限界、日本の役割 
安全性をめぐる企業間協調は機能するか 
OpenAI、Anthropic、Google DeepMind による協調は、安全対策を共通の競争条件とし、個社
だけが不利益を負う問題を緩和する可能性がある。ただし、協調の対象によって実現可能性は
異なる。能力評価の手法、第三者検証、インシデント報告などの情報共有や共通基準づくりは
比較的進めやすい。一方、開発ペースそのものの合意は競争を直接制約するため、抜け駆けの
誘因が残るほか、独占禁止法（反トラスト法）に抵触するおそれもある。実際、米国では、開
発減速をめぐる各社の呼びかけが違法な競争制限に当たるとして、消費者による集団訴訟が提
起されている12。 
前章で示した協調を阻む構造を踏まえると、企業間協調が機能するには、①主要企業の参加、
②検証の信頼性、③副作用の抑制という三つの条件が求められる。 
一つ目の主要企業の参加では、主要な開発主体が協調に参加し、不参加企業との競争に対処
できることが求められる。不参加主体が開発を続ければ、参加企業にも安全策を緩める誘因が
生じるため、協調の参加範囲を広げるとともに、不参加主体との競争によって合意が崩れない
仕組みが必要となる。 
二つ目の検証の信頼性では、合意した安全策の実施状況を相互に確認できることが求められ
る。他社が合意を守っていると確認できなければ、自社だけが不利益を負うことへの警戒から、
協調は維持されにくい。ただし、最先端モデルを評価できる主体は限られるほか、評価期間の

短縮や、評価中のモデルが評価の意図を認識して行動を変える可能性など、検証の信頼性を損
なう要因もある。 
三つ目の副作用の抑制では、協調が市場集中や競争制限につながることを防ぐ必要がある。
前章でみたように、高度で費用のかかる安全基準は既存大手への集中を強めかねない。また、
政府や評価機関が主要企業の知見や資源に依存すれば、企業の利益が過度に制度に反映される
おそれもある。このため、基準形成の透明性や外部主体の関与を確保し、安全性を理由とする
協調が既存企業の地位を守り、市場競争を制限する手段とならないようにする必要がある。 
企業間の自主的な合意だけでは、不参加企業に網をかけられず、検証の独立性や基準づくり
の透明性も確保しにくい。このため、三つの条件を満たしていくには、企業間で形成された安
全基準や評価方法を各国の制度に取り込み、国際的な枠組みを通じて共有する必要がある 。国
家間で開発減速に合意するなど、開発ペースそのものを調整することは難しくとも、能力評価
やインシデント報告の基準を共有し、各国が共通の基準に基づいて企業の対応を検証する仕組
みは構築し得る。 
こうした仕組みの実効性を確保するには、企業間で形成された基準を国際的に共有するだけ
でなく、各国の言語や利用環境において安全策が機能するかを確認し、その実施状況を独立し
た立場から検証できる主体を増やす必要がある。

日本は国際的な枠組みづくりで存在感を示せるか 
最先端 AI の開発を米国企業などが先導する中でも、日本が AI の安全性をめぐる国際的な枠
組みの形成に関与する余地は残されている。日本は、2023 年 5 月の G7 広島サミットを契機に
立ち上げられた広島 AI プロセスを通じて、高度な AI を開発する組織に求める国際指針や行動
規範の形成を主導してきた13。広島 AI プロセスでは、国際行動規範への対応状況を各組織が自
主的に報告・公表する「報告枠組み」も運用されており、各社のリスク管理や安全対策を国際
的に可視化・比較するための基盤が形成されている 14。ただし、企業間で形成された安全基準
を各国の制度へ接続する段階では、こうした原則や枠組みを提唱した実績だけで、日本が引き
続き国際的な枠組み形成に実質的に関与できるとは限らない。 
前節で整理したとおり、企業間で形成された安全基準や評価方法を国際的な枠組みへ発展さ
せるには、各国の言語や利用環境における実効性を 独立した立場から検証できる主体を増やす
必要がある。評価・検証に必要なのは、評価対象モデルへのアクセス、評価手法や評価環境、
専門人材であり、自国で最先端AI を開発する能力を有することは、必ずしも前提とならない。
日本が国際的な枠組みの形成に実質的に関与するには、まずは最先端AI の能力・リスクに関す
る評価結果や、安全対策の実効性を自ら技術的に検証できる体制を整えることが重要となる。 
日本には、その足掛かりとなる実績や知見がある。日本政府が 2024 年に設立した、AI の安

13 広島 AI プロセス「広島AI プロセスについて」（2026 年10 月5 日最終閲覧） 
14 広島 AI プロセス「報告枠組み」（2026 年10 月5 日最終閲覧）

全性に関する評価や基準策定を担う機関、AI セーフティ・インスティテュート（AISI）15は、
10 か国が参加するAI モデルの国際共同テスト演習に加わり、複数の言語におけるLLM（大規模
言語モデル）の評価手法や AI エージェントの評価手法の改善に取り組んできた16。日本が知見
を蓄積しつつある領域である。
このように、日本には評価・検証の足掛かりとなる実績があるものの、それを本格的に担う
ための体制整備はこれからである。AISI はこれまで、評価基準やガイドといった「物差しづく
り」を重視してきた。2025 年の年次レポートでは、今後の方向性として「自ら評価する能力を
もつ」ことと「国際協調を主導する」ことを掲げている17。日本政府も 2026 年 7 月に改定・閣
議決定した「人工知能基本計画」において、AISI の抜本的強化を引き続き掲げている。同年 6
月には、将来的に AISI が最先端 AI などを評価できる体制の構築に向け、必要な手法や評価環
境を検討する調査が開始された18。

日本に期待されるのは、企業間で形成された安全基準をそのまま受け入れるのではなく、各
国の言語や利用環境における実効性を検証し、国際的な枠組みへ接続する際の橋渡し役である。
そのためには、AISI の専門人材や評価環境の充実に加え、公開前を含む評価対象モデルへのア
クセス、機微情報を扱うための情報管理体制、他国のAI 安全機関との共同評価体制を整える必
要がある。AI の能力向上と企業間協調をめぐる議論が急速に進む中、最先端 AI を自ら評価す
る日本の体制づくりはなお調査・検討段階にある。このままでは、最先端 AI の開発だけでな
く、その安全性をめぐる国際的な枠組みの形成でも後れを取りかねない。広島AI プロセスで培
った実績を活かし、国際的な枠組みづくりに継続的に関与していくためにも、最先端 AI の能
力・リスクに関する評価結果や安全対策の実効性を、自ら技術的に検証できる体制を早急に整
えることが求められる。
以上

## Document 172: Allianz_2026-10-09_Toasted, not burnt France s bond sell-off and the road to the 2027 election_99a3202d

Source PDF: all report pdf/Allianz_2026-10-09_Toasted, not burnt France s bond sell-off and the road to the 2027 election_99a3202d.pdf
Report source: Allianz Trade
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

In Summary 
• France is the hardest hit in the global bond sell-off, mostly due to its own fiscal and political 
weakness. Since the Hormuz crisis began, 10-year OAT yields have risen 150bps (US +115bps, 
Italy +132bps, Germany +83bps) as the prolonged energy crisis exacerbates fiscal concerns in 
a politically fragile environment. Franc e’ fiscal deficits have remained above 5% of GDP for 
four consecutive years. Debt has risen from 98% of GDP in 2019 to over 119% in Q2 2026, and 
the 2026 deficit is now expected at -5.4% against a -5% initial target. 
• A "short Europe" trade amplifies the move and spreads it to the rest of the Eurozone while 
the ECB looks the other way. Foreign banks and non-banks hold 36% of OATs and Japanese 
investors about 5% (EUR137bn), exposing France to the repatriation trade and price-sensitive 
investors. Speculative shorts, hedge -fund asset -swap unwinds and thin liquidity feed a 
negative loop, w ith market depth more than 50% below normal and the OAT spread over 
Bunds peaking at 150bp s. The speed reflects a positioning shock, not a pure repricing of 
fundamentals. Long -term investors are sitting out ahead of the elections, given budget 
uncertainty and the sensitivity of OATs to political fragility. Spanish and Italian spreads are 
about 20bps wider, bank CDS are widening and French and Italian three -month bills are 
converging toward the ECB's marginal lending rate. Fragmentation is far from 2012 or 2020 
levels, but the ingredients are in place. The ECB has not yet intervened and may limit  itself to 
verbal intervention, while continuing QT and pushing through two more hikes (+50bps).  
• Baseline: A watered -down budget, but the fiscal arithmetic stays heavy for France .  
We expect GDP growth of +0.5% in 2026 and +0.9% in 2027. The Assembly votes on the budget 
on 20 and 27 October, and we expect a budget by year -end through ordinances or Article 
49.3, keeping the 2027 deficit at -5.3% of GDP. This should contain fiscal slippage and stabilize 
financial conditions, with OAT spreads in the 120 -150bps range. Higher rates  (+100bps) will 
feed through over the next three to six months, adding EUR25bn (0.8% of GDP) in gross 
interest expenses for households, non -financial firms and the government combined. The 
debt-stabilizing deficit is -2.7% of GDP, which requires savings of more than  EUR80bn, plus 
EUR15-20bn a year to stop social and interest spending from widening the deficit. This takes 
credible fiscal measures (better tax mix, higher retirement age) and growth -enhancing 
reforms. 
• Adverse scenarios: France-centered crisis or Eurozone crisis? The Middle East situation is far 
from contained; oil prices could stay above USD100/bbl for another six months , leading to a 
further global bond sell -off hitting France the hardest, tightening financial conditions and 
jeopardizing deficit reduction. Domestically, opposition parties could take down the 
government or slippage surprises resurface despite a passed budget, tightening conditions in 
the same way. In this context, GDP would stall at +0.0% in 2027 and spread s reach 150 -
175bps. The ECB would talk up and potentially launch repo intervention. In our view, it would 
take a substantial Eurozone -wide contraction (France -0.5% in 2027 ; Eurozone -0.2%; OAT 
spreads at 175 -200bps) for the ECB to tweak or stop QT, launch open market and repo 
intervention and stabilize funding markets (as OATs and BTPs make up 50% of euro repo 
collateral). 
• Looking ahead, the 2027 elections are the next test, and TPI is a late and uncertain 
backstop. With the presidential election on 18 A pril and 2 May 2027 (legislative elections 
likely to follow), we see four scenarios: i) a President with an absolute majority and allies, who 
delivers fiscal consolidation; ii) a President short of a majority who seeks alliances  (our 
baseline, with the 2028 deficit at -4.9% and spreads above 100bps) ; iii) a President short of a 
majority without alliances  and iv) a political and fiscal crisis, for instance from unfunded tax 
cuts or spending hikes, with spreads above 200bps and widespread contagion. Only in t his 
last scenario would the ECB activate the Transmission Protection Instrument (TPI). The risk is 
a lack of political will rather than of instruments, and redenomination risk is the tail case.

Toasted, not burnt: France’s bond sell-off  
and the road to the 2027 election

### Page 2

France is the country most affected in the global bond sell-off

Since the start of the Hormuz crisis,  yields on 10 -year French government bonds (OAT) have risen by 150bps  
(vs 115bps for the US, 132bps for Italy and 83bps for Germany), the highest jump among developed economies. 
The feedback loop between rising oil prices and interest rate expectations  has met structurally vulnerable public 
finances whose credibility has been increasingly undermined by growing political fragility since the 2024 dissolution 
of the National Assembly. France’s debt-to-GDP ratio has surged from 98% in 2019 to over 119% in Q2 2026, with 
over 121% of GDP expected in 2027 (vs 83% to 87% in the Eurozone). Meanwhile, the  weakening economy (+0.5% 
GDP growth expected this year)  is leading to a fourth consecutive  year of fiscal slippage : The government now 
expects a deficit of -5.4% of GDP against the -5.1% targeted in the 2026 Budget. With Moody's upcoming review on 
23 October, France is now at risk of losing its AA rating (currently Aa3 by Moody’s vs A+ by S&P and Fitch Ratings, 
i.e. already one notch below); current pricing, if sustained, would even imply a BBB rating.

Table 1: Macroeconomic and fiscal forecasts for France in 2027

Sources: French government, Allianz Research

France is the first victim of the Japanese repatriation trade.  More than other European sovereigns, France's 
investor base is exposed to price -sensitive players. The most volatile group, foreign banks and non -banks, holds 
36% of French government bonds. Domestic banks and non-banks, whose holdings fluctuate less, account for 32%, 
while non -price sensitive investors (domestic and foreign central banks) make up only 32%. Other European 
countries have either a stronger anchor in the domestic market (Italy  with 66% vs 47% for France ) or with foreign 
central banks ( Germany with 26% vs 16 % for France as Bunds as the primary FX reserve vehicle). Among foreign 
holders, Japanese investors play a significant role , holding around 5% of outstanding French government bonds 
(~EUR137bn). As interest rates rise in Japan, these positions become less attractive relative to  domestic holdings. 
At best they are no longer rolled over, at worst they are actively liquidated. In the recent bond sell -off France 
became the first victim of the J apanese repatriation trade, even though it plays only a minor role compared with 
the US as an overweight in Japanese investors' benchmark allocations .

### Page 3

Fiscal concerns, political fragility and investor base have created the vulnerability ; sentiment and positioning 
determine the market reaction. A "short Europe" trade has built up for some time in markets and France is identified 
as the most vulnerable link (Figure 2). A negative feedback loop is now at work. A bearish narrative around political 
fragility and fiscal risk is amplified by very large speculative short positions, sales from Japanese institutional 
investors, hedge fund unwinding asset swap positions and a l imited presence of buyers, with market depth more 
than 50% below normal. In such conditions , even small trades can move prices sharply. OAT yields have spiked 
rapidly, with the 10-year spread vs the Bund widening to a peak 150bps, rising twice as fast as during the Eurozone 
crisis. Realized OAT spread volatility has jumped to the 90th percentile. These market dynamics point to massive 
repositioning rather than a repricing of fundamentals . But as long as a stabilizing force of buyers is missing, this 
volatility can persist.

Figure 2: A Short Europe trade has built up with pressure on EGB spreads and EUR -USD

Sources: LSEG Datastream, Allianz Research

As c ontagion has switched side s, the European Government Bond (EGB) market could move from country -
specific fragility to general fragmentation. The OAT sell-off has already spilled over to other EGBs. Unlike 15 years 
ago, when France fell victim to contagion from the periphery, it is now the other way round. Spanish and Italian 
bond yields rose significantly, while German yields fell. Their 10-year spreads versus the Bund widened by around 
20bps. This decoupling within the EGB space is accompanied by widening bank CDS spreads, especially in France. 
Meanwhile, Euro funding market is under elevated stress. French and Italian three-month bills are converging 
toward the ECB's Marginal Lending Facility rate, the effective ceiling for Eurozone money-market rates. A break 
above the MLF would signal a disruption in funding  markets. The ingredients for a broader fragmentation of 
monetary conditions are thus in place, even if the current magnitude  remains far from those of  2012 or 2020. 
However, monetary transmission is also a function  of time: If these stretched conditions persist for an extended 
period, an intervention of the ECB will be more likely , e specially if we see a persistent decoupling of yield 
movements between core and periphery that can feed fears of redenomination risk.

What’s next?

The 2027 budget bill is facing stiff opposition at the National Assembly, putting the planned deficit reduction in 
jeopardy. Presented to the cabinet on 1 October, the draft budget (PLF) and the social security finance bill (PLFSS) 
for 2027 target a public deficit of -5.0% of GDP, down from -5.4% in 2026. The budget is essentially about preventing 
the deficit from getting worse rather than a fiscal adjustment. The government has announced  EUR43bn of new 
fiscal-measures (1.4% of GDP) , split between EUR25bn (58%) in spending restraint (including slowing spending 
compared to expected trends such as healthcare spending, central government cuts, social spending restraint, local 
authorities cuts) and EUR18bn (42%) in new revenues.

First, opposition parties are already pushing back against most of the big revenue raisers measures, including the

### Page 4

cut to the tax allowance on pensioners’ income (EUR1.4bn), the taxation of sick leave (0.5bn) and cutbacks on 
funding to local governments (EUR2.5bn). Second, the persistence of elevated energy prices and tighter financial 
conditions could pressure GDP growth next year, denting expected government receipts. The government expects 
GDP growth at +1% in 2027, while we e xpect +0.9% but with increasing downside risks. The next key milestones to 
watch will be 20th and 27 October when the National Assembly votes on the Draft budget part I (revenues) and 
the Social Security finance bill (Figure 3). The debt stabilizing fiscal deficit stands at -2.7% of GDP, half of today’s 
level, which entails an improvement of the public deficit of EUR83bn from 2027 on top of around EUR15-20bn of 
additional savings to find each year  just to prevent the deficit from rising  (to contain rising social security and 
interest spending).

Figure 3: France’s key political & budget milestones

Note: legislative elections are not yet scheduled – the National Assembly’s term runs to 2029 unless the next president dissolves 
it. Sources: LOLF art. 45, Constitution art. 47, Assemblée nationale budget calendar of 15 Jul 2026, rating agencies, Allianz  
Research

We lay out different scenarios of what could happen between now and the elections (Figure 4). Starting in the 
short-term, we envisage three broad possibilities: i) a watered-down budget passed before year-end which plans a 
stabilization or a slight improvement of the deficit, ii) a political crisis  with opposition parties taking down the 
government and iii) a re-intensification of the energy crisis.

Figure 4: Economic, political and market scenarios October 2026-April 2027

Source: Allianz Research 
In our baseline scenario, we  expect the French government will manage to pass a watered-down budget while 
the energy crisis eases,  allowing the deficit to remain in check in 2027 (-5.3% of GDP) Opposition parties do not

### Page 5

have an incentive  to soften their stance with the Presidential elections approaching on 18 April and 2 May 2027. 
But they also do not have an incentive to create a political crisis by taking down the government. Notably, the 
National Rally (Rassemblement National, RN) has expressed a preference for implementing a budget rather than 
relying on the ‘’Special Law’’ mechanism that was used in early 2025 and early 2026 In this environment, we expect 
the government to manage to pass a mini budget, by year -end, of limited fiscal effort just to keep the deficit in 
check (at -5.3% of GDP), either through executive ordinances – a budgetary mechanism never previously utilized 
under the Fifth Republic – or by triggering Article 49.3.  
In this baseline scenario, OAT spreads should enter a phase of stabilization but contagion risk remains. After the 
clean-up of derivative positions, we see 10 -year OAT spreads trading in a pre -election range of 120 -150bps. But 
the evolution of the French spread will not be isolated , with the potential to trigger a renewed contagion move.  
The French budget and election situation feeds the “short Europe trade”, and this negative sentiment dominates 
country-specific fundamentals. We also do not believe that stabilizing the French situation alone will be enough to 
bring the EGB market back into a convergence trade. Now that dislocations have emerged, EGB spreads are likely 
to become the new playground for speculative (leveraged) players looking to exploit them. With this comes 
elevated rates and FX  volatility and the risk of money market stress. We expect the ECB in this case to limit itself 
first to verbal intervention like a change in wording from "monitoring" to "ready to act" or "disorderly market 
dynamics". We see the tipping point at a 10-year OAT spread above 150bps. 
In a more adverse scenario where the energy crisis does not ease and/or France slips into another political crisis, 
a crisis emerges, either France-centered, or with spillovers and contagion to other Eurozone countries.  Under a 
scenario of renewed tensions in energy markets, the global markets selloff would intensify. Financial conditions 
would tighten more in France, weakening the economy and putting in jeopardy the deficit reduction. Alternatively, 
the government could be taken down in Parliament, triggering a grave political crisis and leading to tighter 
financial conditions. Even under our baseline scenario of a watered-down budget, fiscal slippages could re-emerge 
– for example if the economy is weaker than expected, or if the fiscal adjustment fall s short of what is planned  
(e.g. if revenue -raising measures disappoint). Additionally, under a Special Law, the risk of wider deficit could 
increase if continued through the presidential elections  in 2027, which could lead to France -centered crisis or a 
Eurozone crisis if tightening financial conditions in France spill over to other Eurozone countries . In a France -
centered crisis we would expect the French economy t o enter a mid-sized recession as tighter financial conditions 
would weigh on domestic demand (see next section) , with GDP at +0.0% in 2027. In a Eurozone crisis , we would 
expect French GDP to contract -0.5% and the whole Eurozone economy -0.2%.  
In this adverse scenario, we could see the 10-year OAT spread rising to 1 75-200bps. This would create 
widespread contagion in the EGB market and in the banking sector. In this case, it will clearly be up to the ECB not 
to let the situation escalate into a euro crisi s 2.0. Unlike in the euro crisis, when the escalation was fueled by a lack 
of instruments while the political will was there, this time the deployment of the instruments (TPI, OMT) could fail 
due to a lack of political will and institutional inertia. It wo uld be particularly precarious if markets began to price 
redenomination risk, i.e. the possibility of a country leaving the Eurozone. But we see this scenario as a tail risk. If 
the EGB and money markets move towards general fragmentation, we expect the ECB to set operational steps (QT 
tweak, temporary operations) at OAT spreads around 175bps, but only alongside broader contagion with BTP 
spreads around 150bp s and Spain and Portugal widening together. For France, a QT tweak would represent a 
relief of EUR4.8bn on average per month, equivalent to around 20% of monthly issuances since June. But the 
problem is that “Europe short traders” can also do this math and adjust accordingly. At this point the ECB would 
not only operate in the secondary bond market but a lso need to stabilize funding markets as in  French OAT and 
Italian BTP represent 50% of the EUR repo market collateral. The TPI activation would be late in the process and 
require OAT spread around 200bps with widespread and persistent contagion.

In 2027, the presidential (and likely legislative) elections  could re -escalate economic and market pressures.  
We envisage four scenarios, which we detail more in Figure 5. In a nutshell, under our baseline assumptions, the

### Page 6

newly elected President will not secure a n absolute majority when she or he provokes legislative elections.  
Nevertheless, the President will have the strongest group at the National Assembly and will benefit from strong 
popular support. In this environment, we would expect the President to negotiate with some opposition parties to 
pass a budget putting the deficit on a downward trajectory. Nevertheless, deficit reduction will be limited ( -4.9% 
GDP deficit expected in 2028) amid strong aversion for rapid fiscal tightening in Parliament and public opinion. The 
10-year spread will likely remain above 100bps. In an adverse scenario of political and fiscal crisis, for instance 
triggered by the enactment of tax cuts and/or spending hikes  not credibly funded, a France-centered or Eurozone 
crisis scenario would re-emerge. In the latter case, the ECB would likely intervene with the TPI with French spreads 
breaking 200bps and Italian spreads above 175bps.

Figure 5:  Economic, political and market scenarios after April 2027

Source: Allianz Research

In the short term, a significant easing of global yields and elevated French spreads hinges on three factors:   
1) a substantial easing of the energy crisis, which would reduce inflationary pressures, limiting or even ending 
further central bank hikes. 2) Strong and credible fiscal -consolidation measures passed by decree, though this 
remains unlikely before the presidential election. One option used previously is  reducing healthcare spending, but 
the amount of savings is limited to EUR5-10bn (Table 2). 3) A cooling US economy could moderate the global bond 
sell off, though this too seems less likely before 2027.

Table 2: Measures the French government can implement by decrees to bypass parliamentary vote

Measure Can government act without new parliamentary vote? 
Cancel already authorized State spending Yes 
Freeze/cancel some budgetary appropriations Yes 
Adjust medicine reimbursement within regulatory powers Yes 
Adjust healthcare tariffs/payment parameters Yes, within statutory framework 
Tighten implementation of existing social schemes Often yes 
Change parameters of social contribution rebates  Sometimes yes 
Abolish/reduce a statutory benefit Generally, no 
Permanently change tax rates No 
Fundamentally redesign social-contribution exemptions Generally, requires legislation 
Change the ONDAM target itself Generally, Parliament/LFSS

Sources: French government, various, Allianz Research 
In the longer-term, only a sizeable fiscal -consolidation program can ease French spreads sustainably.  
Ideally, a combination of the following would have the most benefits:

### Page 7

Credible fiscal adjustment (the least growth -hurting). On the revenue side, fiscal consolidation should rely on a 
more efficient tax mix to limit negative growth effects. An increase of  VAT rates would bring large receipts without 
hurting potential growth. Freez ing income-tax thresholds should be preferred to increase social contributions to 
limit the negative impact on labor demand and labor supply amid already high labor taxation in France. Some 
inefficient and costly tax rebates and loopholes could also be cut down. But most of the fiscal adjustment should 
be operated through expenditure cuts. An increase of the effective age of retirement – for instance through a rise 
in the number of years of contribution – would bring substantial savings and increase tax receipts. Substantial 
savings could also be enacted on public agencies and local governments’ administrative costs. In parallel, the 
government could launch a comprehensive spending review to improve efficiency  of public expenditure, 
benchmarking what has been done in the past in countries such as Canada and Sweden.  
Growth-enhancing reforms. The government could implement structural reforms which will likely support growth 
in the medium -term, reducing the need for further painful fiscal adjustment later on. A pension reform would not 
only bring higher receipts and increase savings; it would also stimulate labor supply and potential growth 1. Other 
growth-enhancing measures include a revamp of school education met hods, scaling back excessive regulation, 
incentivizing higher-skilled education and hiring through a reform of the taxation system.

The trickle-down effect of tighter financial conditions

The ongoing bond market problem could increasingly become an economic problem through tighter financial 
and credit conditions, but the economy has buffers to sustain a short -term financial shock. Higher sovereign 
interest rates have started to filter through on to consumer, mortgage and corporate funding costs, and the pa ss-
through will continue through 2027. This will tighten the credit and financial cycle, through higher borrowing costs 
on new lending, higher refinancing bills and tighter access to credit, and ultimately weaken consumer and 
investment spending. Our BIS-built Financial condition index (FCI) for France shows that overall financial conditions 
have tightened sharply recently, but from a relatively loose level (Figure 6, left). They are now close to their long -
term historical averages. Besides, the credit impu lse for households and corporations remain s supportive to date 
(Figure 6, right). During the Eurozone crisis of 2011 -13, the credit impulse turned sharply negative but GDP growth 
held up. While the latest ECB Bank Lending Survey (BLS) points to the credit impulses weakening in the next couple 
of quarters, this does not necessarily mean that GDP growth will weaken sharply. The French economy has buffers 
to withstand a negative finance shock, provided it does not last too long and does not get worse. In parti cular, 
households have high savings and corporates’ cash reserves have been rebuilt recently, though many firms struggle 
with elevated bankruptcies.

Figure 6: France’s financial conditions index (left); credit impulses & GDP growth (right)

Sources: LSEG Datastream, BIS, Allianz Research 
Note: last data point in the FCI is 5th October 2026.  
A rise of +100bps of interest rates  mean EUR25bn (0.8% of GDP)  in additional gross interest expenses for the 
French households, non -financial corporates and the government. We find that  a +100bps increase in interest

rates would almost double households’ net interest income in 2026 to -EUR16.3bn from -EUR8.5bn in 2025, or the 
equivalent of 0.8% of disposable income (see Figure 5). Contrary to US or UK households, French households tend 
to lose when interest rates increase. We calculate that the interest earned on assets would reach EUR24.5bn while 
the interest paid would rise to EUR40.7bn.

Figure 7: Impact of a +100bps shock on interest rates for French households

Sources: LSEG Datastream, Allianz Research

For French non-financial companies, around 50.6% of debt is due to reset or mature within the next 12 months, 
well below the level in Italy, Spain, the UK and the US, where 78 to 90% is due for refinancing. However, France 
starts from the largest corporate debt stock in Europe in this sample, at around EUR2.27trn. This means that even 
with a lower refinancing share, around EUR1.15trn of debt still needs to be repriced within a year, more than in 
Germany, It aly o r Spain. In addition, the pressure is amplified by the starting level of borrowing costs. French 
corporates already face an effective interest rate of 6.1%, the highest among the European peers shown here. Under 
a +100bp s interest-rate shock, the effective pass -through would amount to roughly +89bp s, adding around 
EUR10bn to annual interest expenses. Relative to gross operating surplus, this represents an additional burden of 
about 2.1% of GOS, the highest impact across similar peers, and could shave roughl y -0.7pp off gross margins (see 
Figure 6). For the French government, a +100bps increase in the weighted average interest rates would mean +0.3% 
of GDP in 2027 (EUR 11.7bn) and +0.7% of GDP by 2035. Indeed, France has a rather long average maturity of its 
debt (>8.5 years) which protects it from very high volatility on its interest expenses.

ECONOMIC RESEARCH
